# Implementation Log

> **Prompt:** "Merge the WooPayments client into WooCommerce core as a truly native payments runtime (Option C), end-to-end, with no human in the loop. Work in ~/Work/a8c/woocommerce-develop-2 on the current branch (exp/core-native-payments). Status: A0 is built, validated, and committed. Produce a just-in-time task plan for A1, then implement A1->A6 in order, planning each stage just before doing it."

> **Prompt:** "Continue working toward the active thread goal."

> **Prompt:** "Remember that WPCOM code changes are off limits. Equaly important, remember that you must NEVER commit or push to trunk on the WPCOM codebase."

> **Prompt:** "Make sure that the WooPayments checkout surfaces (classic or block based) factor in whether the current WooPayments connected account is a test mode account (it should surface specific details about test cards and test badge - check the reference checkout). This behavior needs to be maintained - like I said, no merchant or shopper facing regressions or functionality gaps are allowed."

> **Prompt:** "Also, why isn't http://store8889.localhost:8889/wp-admin/admin.php?page=wc-settings&tab=checkout loading with the providers list (including WooPayments) like http://localhost:8082/wp-admin/admin.php?page=wc-settings&tab=checkout is? Are you absolutely sure it is a limitation of the http://store8889.localhost:8889/ local env or is it something we broke? The frontend UX of the WooCommerce > Settings > Payments page should not be affected by our changes - it needs to work as it used to work because it is not WooPayments specific (WooPayments is a provider just like any other, even if now it is a WC native provider). Think deeply about how we wire that in in a proper way."

> **Prompt:** "Do the WooPay buttons get the proper styling that they do on the reference store?"

> **Prompt:** "Having a button that is not properly styled is unacceptable regression."

> **Prompt:** "When we are implementing frontend work, CSS styling needs to be factored in and properly handled, on equal footing - the merge into core can't be only about functionality."

> **Prompt:** "Make sure you keep things bundled separately so the core maintains its frontend performance and adaptability to actually enabled features (e.g. WooPay may be disabled by the merchant)"

- 2026-06-15 19:07: Created session log before synthesizing findings.
- 2026-06-16 07:07: B2af review follow-up: verified the review finding against WooPayments' `WCPay\MultiCurrency\Utils::is_call_in_backtrace()`. WooPayments compares against `wp_debug_backtrace_summary()` strings such as `WCS_Cart_Renewal->setup_cart`, `WC_Cart_Totals->calculate_item_totals`, `WC_Discounts->apply_coupon`, and `WC_Product->get_price`. The native `MultiCurrencySubscriptionsCompatibilityController::is_call_in_backtrace()` reconstructed every class call as `Class::method`, so instance-method guards for subscription product, coupon, and recurring-item conversion would miss production backtraces. Fix requires a failing regression that exercises real `debug_backtrace()` formatting, then preserving the `type` operator from the backtrace frame.
- 2026-06-16 07:59: B2aj selection: continue the B2af deferred Subscriptions compatibility surface with direct `woocommerce_subscriptions_product_price` and sign-up fee conversion. Read-only WooPayments reference shows three missing frontend filters at priority 50: `woocommerce_subscriptions_product_price`, `woocommerce_product_get__subscription_sign_up_fee`, and `woocommerce_product_variation_get__subscription_sign_up_fee`. The native controller will reuse `MultiCurrencyPriceProjectionService::get_price()` for product conversion and preserve WooPayments' switch-cart guards for `WC_Subscriptions_Cart::set_subscription_prices_for_calculation`, repeated `WC_Subscriptions_Product::get_sign_up_fee` during `WC_Cart->calculate_totals`, `WCS_Switch_Totals_Calculator` apportioning, and already-mutated `_subscription_sign_up_fee` meta. My Account formatted subscription totals remain a later slice because they need current subscription currency state and explicit `wc_price` formatting context. WPCOM remains off limits: no WPCOM code changes, commits, or pushes.

Fix direction is to add branded button markup and WooPay-specific CSS through the Core WooPayments/WooPay asset path, not global checkout CSS, so stores with WooPay disabled avoid WooPay-specific frontend weight.

- 2026-06-16 17:51: A3f code review gate initially returned request-changes. Russell found that zero-total saved-token checkout skipped setup-capable providers and that immediate native recurring successes could complete while token-save failures were swallowed. Kepler found the related integration gap that recurring direct native checkout also needed `setup_future_usage=off_session` before creating the PaymentIntent. Fixed by teaching `PaymentProcessingService` to treat saved WooCommerce tokens as zero-total setup credentials, by forcing `setup_future_usage` for recurring native charge requests, and by returning `wcpay_recurring_token_save_failed` failed outcomes when recurring token persistence fails in the immediate adapter path. - 2026-06-17 01:33: B3aa root cause confirmed for the Settings Payments provider-list regression: it was native Core wiring, not a `store8889.localhost:8889` environment limitation. Native Settings onboarding still depended on plugin-owned `/wc/v3/payments/onboarding/*` routes and had wrong WPCOM transport shape for onboarding fields; recommendations were missing because native `NativeWooPaymentsGateway` did not implement the provider recommendation contract; the public recommendations URL initially bypassed Jetpack's WPCOM API base and local wpcom-local routing; and native onboarding fields lacked the plugin/reference `__locale` and `available_countries` keys when the WooPayments plugin runtime was absent. The fix routes native onboarding through `WooPaymentsApiClient` with connection-owner user-token support where the plugin reference uses it, keeps legacy REST fallback for plugin-owned runtime, adds public recommendation reads plus transient caching, and fills onboarding field locale/countries from Core-owned WooPayments country data.

- 2026-06-17 02:52: B3ac selection: restore WooPayments order-tracking queue continuity. The reference plugin registers `woocommerce_update_order` on the main gateway, schedules preserved `wcpay_track_new_order` and `wcpay_track_update_order` Action Scheduler hooks when Sift is enabled and a WooPayments order has `_payment_method_id`, and consumes those hooks through `WC_Payments_API_Client::track_order()` at `tracking/order`. Native currently has the generic Action Scheduler wrapper and webhook reliability consumers, but no producer/consumer for the two order-tracking hooks and no native API client method for `tracking/order`. - 2026-06-16 01:37: Began B2 planning. Confirmed that `MultiCurrencyRuntimeArbiter` already couples multi-currency ownership to `NativePaymentsRuntimeArbiter`: plugin payments ownership keeps plugin-MC active only when `_wcpay_feature_customer_multi_currency` is enabled, native payments ownership flips MC to core, and no payments owner leaves MC dormant. WooCommerce bootstrap currently registers only read-only MC shadow mode; the payments runtime already has native-only registries/controllers. Current B1 MC services expose read-only hook/asset/route manifests for compatibility, async pricing, settings, Storefront, My Account user settings, admin notices, WC Admin notes, and REST, while frontend price/currency projection remains behavior-only. The narrow B2a slice will therefore add a core MC registration coordinator/manifest that consumes the arbiter decision and reports all core MC hook groups as blocked in plugin or no-owner modes, without registering price filters or moving frontend assets yet.

Native already has the compatibility filters in `MultiCurrencyStateBuilder` and price projection, and B2f added `MultiCurrencyRequestContext::is_store_batch_request()` and `is_store_api_request()`. B2g should add a small request override controller that registers these request-local filters for non-Store REST only, with no persisted writes and no WPCOM/WooPayments source edits.

## Extracted Constraints

### Operating Model

- Execute with no human-in-the-loop; decide from docs, code, and local evidence.
- Use two local stores as the parity oracle: `http://store8889.localhost:8889` for the target native runtime work and `http://localhost:8082` for the reference unmodified WooPayments behavior.
- Treat every gate as fail-closed. No stage advances with a red gate or an undispositioned touched BC surface.
- Record gate evidence in the staging log for every stage.

### A0 Current State

- A0 is committed at starting HEAD `4e8c66d3f2486c45f054cb3eea79619afeaf681e`.
- Built A0 deliverables include `NativePaymentsRuntimeArbiter`, the BC drift gate, Bucket-E surface dump/parity primitive, perf baseline/check, financial reconciliation, Tracks capture/normalization/parity tooling, `flow-drive.sh`, and `verify.sh`.
- The BC facades scaffold is deferred to A1/A2 because there was no inward target before the native skeleton exists.
- The A5 cutover component is specified but intentionally not built in A0.

### A1-Specific Constraints

- A1 creates `src/Internal/Payments/` skeletons for `OrderPaymentStore`, `PaymentContext`, `PaymentOutcome`, `ProviderContract`, and `CapabilityManifest`, plus the `src/Internal/Payments/Providers/WooPayments/` skeleton.
- A1 must wire read-only shadow mode that records native-computed effects after plugin-owned processing.
- Harness honesty issue to resolve in the A1 task plan: A0's `parity-diff.sh` compares persisted state across two processing stores; it does not prove native-computed-vs-plugin-actual one-store shadow parity.
- A1 must explicitly choose the shadow comparison shape and the shadow subject. At skeleton stage, meaningful shadow parity is expected to start with the read/store layer and strengthen in A2/A3.

### Architecture Constraints

- The target is Option C: internal provider platform with a first-party WooPayments provider. Public 3PD API promotion is committed but sequenced off the critical path and gated after the internal contract proves itself.
- The runtime arbiter rule remains: plugin active means plugin wins; native must register nothing mutating until the plugin is deactivated and native is enabled.
- Provider-specific payload shapes must stop at the WooPayments provider adapter. Generic payments code consumes neutral context/outcome/event/store abstractions only.
- Perf claims must be falsifiable per surface. The A0 perf probe is narrow and does not prove broad RULE-1 compliance.

### BC Constraints Relevant To A1

- Hard-preserve Bucket E rows include gateway IDs, token type IDs, order/refund meta strings, customer/user meta strings, gateway settings option `woocommerce_woocommerce_payments_settings`, NOX options, inbound server/mobile routes, and surviving Tracks events.
- `OrderPaymentStore` must use WC CRUD / HPOS-safe APIs, not raw post-meta.
- Exact persisted key strings to encode first include: `_intent_id`, `_payment_method_id`, `_charge_id`, `_intention_status`, `_charge_risk_level`, `_stripe_customer_id`, `_wcpay_intent_currency`, `_wcpay_refund_id`, `_wcpay_refund_transaction_id`, `_wcpay_refund_status`, `_wcpay_transaction_fee`, `_wcpay_mode`, `_wcpay_payment_transaction_id`, `_wcpay_payment_method_details`, `_wcpay_ipp_channel`, `_wcpay_net`, `_stripe_mandate_id`, `is_woopay`, `last4`, and `_card_brand`.
- Surviving Tracks surfaces preserve event names plus meaningful props contract; volatile values and standard Tracks envelope are normalized out.

### Harness Bounds

- Deterministic gates: BC/Tracks drift, sampled Bucket-E persisted state, refund-only financial reconciliation, sink-based Tracks for driven flows, and a narrow gateway-resolution perf probe.
- Runbook/judgment surfaces remain browser checkout including 3DS/SCA, admin screens, bundle size, broad perf, captures/disputes/payouts/fees, subscription renewals, token/customer meta, and multi-currency stored state.
- A1 must label checks as automated-deterministic or runbook-judgment.

### A2-Specific Constraints

- Native may own order lifecycle effects and event ingestion only when the arbiter reports native ownership. While the WooPayments plugin is active, the plugin continues to own the route, queue consumers, and order effects.
- Preserve the in-flight REST route identity: `POST /wc/v3/payments/webhook`, with `manage_woocommerce` permission semantics and the WooPayments-compatible `{ "result": "success" | "bad_request" | "error" }` response envelope.
- Preserve Action Scheduler identity for webhook reliability: group `woocommerce_payments`, hooks `wcpay_webhook_fetch_events` and `wcpay_webhook_process_event`, transient key pattern `wcpay_failed_event_<md5>`, and duplicate-pending-action avoidance.
- Do not add a global core `WC_Payments` facade while the standalone plugin can coexist. Reduce static/god-class usage through namespaced internal services that proxy legacy plugin behavior only when that runtime owns the site.
- Event-to-order resolution must keep the multisite `order_key` guard: a webhook payload that names an order ID with a mismatched key must not mutate that order.

## 2026-06-18 H22 Express Checkout Tracks Continuity

- 2026-06-18 02:48: Selected H22 as the next high-throughput slice after H21. H16 deliberately left non-WooPay express checkout Tracks out of scope until native ECE existed; H18-H20 now own Apple Pay, Google Pay, Amazon Pay, order-pay, and product-page ECE in Core, so the residual Tracks contract can be closed without tying it to WooPay or card rendering.
- 2026-06-18 02:49: Wrote the H22 plan to `.agents/scratchpad/sessions/2026-06-15-core-native-payments/plans/2026-06-18-core-native-payments-h22-express-tracks-continuity.md`. Zeno verified the reference contract: client emits only `applepay_button_load`, `gpay_button_load`, `applepay_button_click`, and `gpay_button_click` through `platform_tracks` with `{ source }`; valid source values are `product`, `cart`, `checkout`, and `pay_for_order`; the server prefixes `wcpay_`; Amazon Pay has no reference tracking event. Epicurus mapped the native gaps: classic over-emitted both wallet events, Blocks load tracking was not method-specific, and express params lacked tracking-enabled flags.
- 2026-06-18 02:52: RED gates passed as expected. `WooPaymentsExpressCheckoutServiceTest` failed on missing `isShopperTrackingEnabled`; classic ECE Jest failed because Apple-only ready and Google click both emitted two requests; Blocks ECE Jest failed because Apple load recorded despite `availablePaymentMethods.applePay=false` and disabled shopper tracking still fetched.
- 2026-06-18 02:55: GREEN implementation added the express checkout tracking-enabled flags from `WooPaymentsFrontendTrackingController`, restricted classic load events to truthy `availablePaymentMethods.applePay/googlePay`, restricted classic click tracking to `event.expressPaymentType`, made Blocks ready tracking method-specific, and passed express params tracking flags into the Blocks `recordWooPaymentsUserEvent()` helper. No Amazon Pay tracking event was added.
- 2026-06-18 02:58: Focused gates passed: PHP `WooPaymentsExpressCheckoutServiceTest` passed with 21 tests and 70 assertions; related PHP `WooPaymentsExpressCheckoutServiceTest|WooPaymentsExpressCheckoutControllerTest|WooPaymentsFrontendTrackingControllerTest` passed with 40 tests and 155 assertions; classic ECE Jest passed with 20 tests; Blocks ECE Jest passed with 11 tests; `php -l` passed for touched PHP files; production PHPStan passed for `WooPaymentsExpressCheckoutService.php`; `lint:php:changes`, targeted classic ESLint for the touched express checkout files, focused Blocks JS lint for touched WooPayments files, normal `@woocommerce/classic-assets` build, normal `@woocommerce/block-library` build, `git diff --check`, and branch `lint:changes:branch` passed. Branch JS lint still emitted existing ignored-file warnings; broad classic asset lint remains blocked by unrelated existing max-len/console issues outside H22.
- 2026-06-18 03:00: Chrome DevTools MCP timed out on `list_pages` after 120 seconds, so H22 does not claim live wallet browser request capture. This is acceptable for this narrow telemetry slice because deterministic PHP/Jest tests prove config, method-specific request names/payloads, disabled-tracking no-op, and the server prefix contract. Timing was not used as evidence.
- 2026-06-18 03:00: Final review agents approved the uncommitted diff. Singer found no API-contract issues and confirmed no Amazon event was invented. James found no new entrypoints, no bundle-scope broadening, no WooPay/card coupling, no event storms, and no disabled-tracking fetches.

The corrected query intentionally still catches legacy prefixed or missing payment-method data.
